#' Run BGNLM Calibration Suite and Select Optimal Configuration
execute_bgnlm_tuning <- function(cores = 2L, fast_calibration = TRUE) {
  candidates <- get_bgnlm_tuning_candidates()
  cal_systems <- c("sys02_lorenz63", "sys08_rossler_lorenz_hybrid", "sys04_lotka_volterra")
  cal_noises <- c(0.01, 0.25)
  cal_N <- if (fast_calibration) c(250L) else c(250L, 1000L)
  cal_seeds <- if (fast_calibration) c(101L) else c(101L, 102L)

  rows <- list()
  for (cand in candidates) {
    message("[TUNING] Evaluating configuration: ", cand$config_id, " (", cand$label, ")")
    for (sys_id in cal_systems) {
      for (n_obs in cal_N) {
        for (eta in cal_noises) {
          for (sd_val in cal_seeds) {
            task_data <- generate_synthetic_task_data(
              system_id = sys_id,
              N_train = n_obs,
              noise_eta = eta,
              noise_regime = "derivative_snr",
              seed = sd_val,
              T_max = 15.0
            )
            N_num <- as.numeric(n_obs)
            p_cov <- ncol(task_data$X_train)
            beta_p <- if (cand$beta_prior_type == "g-prior") {
              list(type = "g-prior", g = max(N_num, p_cov^2))
            } else if (cand$beta_prior_type == "Jeffreys-BIC") {
              list(type = "Jeffreys-BIC", var = cand$var)
            } else {
              list(type = cand$beta_prior_type)
            }
            r_val <- switch(
              cand$r_rule,
              "N^(-0.5)" = N_num^(-0.5),
              "N^(-1.0)" = N_num^(-1.0),
              "N^(-1.5)" = N_num^(-1.5),
              "exp(-2)"  = exp(-2.0),
              "exp(-4)"  = exp(-4.0),
              1.0 / N_num
            )
            override <- cand
            override$beta_prior <- beta_p
            override$model_prior <- list(r = r_val)

            method_call <- if (cand$config_id == "C08_IWSM_Baseline_FixedVar1") "BGNLM_IWSM_Untuned" else "BGNLM_Full_Tuned"
            t0 <- proc.time()[3]
            fit_res <- run_fbms_method(
              method_id = method_call,
              task_data = task_data,
              cores = cores,
              tuning_override = override,
              fast_mode = fast_calibration
            )
            elapsed <- proc.time()[3] - t0

            st_m <- compute_structure_metrics(fit_res$discovered, task_data$system$ground_truth)
            pip_brier <- compute_pip_brier_score(fit_res$pip_list, task_data$system$ground_truth)
            pr_ood <- compute_prediction_metrics(fit_res$pred_ood, task_data$Y_test_ood_clean, fit_res$lower_ood, fit_res$upper_ood)

            rows[[length(rows) + 1L]] <- data.table(
              config_id = cand$config_id,
              label = cand$label,
              beta_prior_type = cand$beta_prior_type,
              r_rule = cand$r_rule,
              p_proj = cand$p_proj,
              p_inter = cand$p_inter,
              system_id = sys_id,
              N_train = n_obs,
              noise_eta = eta,
              seed = sd_val,
              TPR = st_m$TPR,
              FDR = st_m$FDR,
              F1 = st_m$F1,
              Exact_Recovery = st_m$Exact_Recovery,
              SHD = st_m$SHD,
              PIP_Brier = pip_brier,
              R2_ood = pr_ood$R2_clipped,
              Runtime_s = elapsed
            )
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }

  dt_all <- rbindlist(rows)
  summary_dt <- dt_all[, .(
    Mean_F1 = mean(F1, na.rm = TRUE),
    Mean_TPR = mean(TPR, na.rm = TRUE),
    Mean_FDR = mean(FDR, na.rm = TRUE),
    Exact_Rate = mean(Exact_Recovery, na.rm = TRUE),
    Mean_SHD = mean(SHD, na.rm = TRUE),
    Mean_PIP_Brier = mean(PIP_Brier, na.rm = TRUE),
    Mean_R2_ood = mean(R2_ood, na.rm = TRUE),
    Mean_Runtime_s = mean(Runtime_s, na.rm = TRUE)
  ), by = .(config_id, label, beta_prior_type, r_rule, p_proj, p_inter)]

  # Composite selection score: 0.50 * Mean_F1 + 0.20 * Exact_Rate + 0.20 * Mean_R2_ood - 0.10 * Mean_PIP_Brier
  summary_dt[, Composite_Score := 0.50 * Mean_F1 + 0.20 * Exact_Rate + 0.20 * Mean_R2_ood - 0.10 * fifelse(is.na(Mean_PIP_Brier), 0.25, Mean_PIP_Brier)]
  setorder(summary_dt, -Composite_Score)

  dir.create("results/aggregated", recursive = TRUE, showWarnings = FALSE)
  dir.create("results/tables", recursive = TRUE, showWarnings = FALSE)
  fwrite(dt_all, "results/aggregated/tuning_runs_raw.csv")
  fwrite(summary_dt, "results/aggregated/tuning_summary.csv")

  best_id <- summary_dt$config_id[1]
  best_cand <- candidates[[which(vapply(candidates, function(x) x$config_id == best_id, logical(1)))[1]]]
  yaml::write_yaml(best_cand, "config/best_bgnlm_params.yaml")
  message("[TUNING] Locked optimal configuration: ", best_cand$config_id, " -> config/best_bgnlm_params.yaml")

  # Export LaTeX booktabs table of tuning ablation
  tex_lines <- c(
    "\\begin{table}[t]",
    "\\centering",
    "\\caption{Systematic BGNLM Hyperparameter and Prior Tuning on the Calibration Suite.}",
    "\\label{tab:bgnlm_tuning}",
    "\\small",
    "\\begin{tabular}{llcccccc}",
    "\\toprule",
    "Config ID & Parameter Prior & $r$ Penalty & $p_{\\text{proj}}$ & F1 $\\uparrow$ & FDR $\\downarrow$ & PIP Brier $\\downarrow$ & $R^2_{\\text{OOD}}$ $\\uparrow$ \\\\",
    "\\midrule"
  )
  for (i in seq_len(nrow(summary_dt))) {
    r <- summary_dt[i]
    cfg_clean <- gsub("_", "\\\\_", r$config_id)
    prior_clean <- gsub("_", "\\\\_", r$beta_prior_type)
    tex_lines <- c(tex_lines, sprintf(
      "%s & %s & \\texttt{%s} & %.2f & %.3f & %.3f & %.3f & %.3f \\\\",
      cfg_clean, prior_clean, r$r_rule, r$p_proj,
      r$Mean_F1, r$Mean_FDR, r$Mean_PIP_Brier, r$Mean_R2_ood
    ))
  }
  tex_lines <- c(tex_lines, "\\bottomrule", "\\end{tabular}", "\\end{table}")
  writeLines(tex_lines, "results/tables/table_bgnlm_tuning.tex")

  list(raw = dt_all, summary = summary_dt, best = best_cand)
}



=== FILE: R/08_mixed_effects_eval.R ===
# ==============================================================================
# R/08_mixed_effects_eval.R
# Mixed-Effects Statistical Evaluation Framework (lme4, lmerTest, glmmTMB, emmeans)
# ==============================================================================

suppressPackageStartupMessages({
  library(lme4)
  library(lmerTest)
  library(glmmTMB)
  library(emmeans)
  library(broom.mixed)
  library(data.table)
})

#' Ensure Method factor has BGNLM_Full_Tuned as reference baseline level
prepare_mixed_model_data <- function(df, ref_method = "BGNLM_Full_Tuned") {
  dt <- as.data.table(copy(df))
  all_methods <- unique(as.character(dt$Method))
  if (ref_method %in% all_methods) {
    dt[, Method := relevel(factor(Method), ref = ref_method)]
  } else {
    dt[, Method := factor(Method)]
  }
  dt
}

#' Map raw checkpoint Method IDs to transparent publication labels
relabel_method_str <- function(x) {
  x <- as.character(x)
  map <- c(
    "SR_PySR" = "SR_Pool_Pareto",
    "SR_Operon" = "SR_Pool_MDL",
    "UQ_SINDy_Oracle" = "Bag_STLSQ_Oracle",
    "UQ_SINDy_Poly" = "Bag_STLSQ_Poly",
    "SINDy_PI_Rational" = "SINDy_RatPoly2"
  )
  for (k in names(map)) {
    x <- gsub(k, map[[k]], x, fixed = TRUE)
  }
  x
}

#' Compute Intraclass Correlation Coefficients (ICC) from a fitted lme4 model
extract_random_effects_icc <- function(fit_mer, model_label = "LMM") {
  vc <- as.data.frame(lme4::VarCorr(fit_mer))
  total_var <- sum(vc$vcov, na.rm = TRUE)
  if (!is.finite(total_var) || total_var <= 0) total_var <- 1.0
  is_sing <- lme4::isSingular(fit_mer)
  data.table(
    Model = model_label,
    Group = vc$grp,
    Variance = vc$vcov,
    StdDev = vc$sdcor,
    ICC = vc$vcov / total_var,
    Singular = ifelse(is_sing, "Yes", "No")
  )
}

#' Merge Dunnett-adjusted p-values onto tidy fixed-effects table
attach_dunnett_pvals <- function(td, emm) {
  td[, p.dunnett := NA_real_]
  if (is.null(emm) || nrow(emm) == 0L) return(td)
  for (i in seq_len(nrow(td))) {
    trm <- as.character(td$term[i])
    if (startsWith(trm, "Method")) {
      m_lvl <- sub("^Method", "", trm)
      hit <- emm[grepl(paste0("^", m_lvl, "\\b"), contrast) | contrast == paste0(m_lvl, " - BGNLM_Full_Tuned")]
      if (nrow(hit) >= 1L && "p.value" %in% names(hit)) {
        td[i, p.dunnett := hit$p.value[1L]]
      }
    }
  }
  td
}

#' Fit Linear Mixed-Effects Model (LMM) cleanly with random-effects fallback and singularity logging
safe_fit_lmm <- function(formula_full, formula_simple, data, label = "LMM") {
  fit <- tryCatch(
    lmerTest::lmer(formula_full, data = data, REML = TRUE),
    error = function(e) NULL
  )
  if (is.null(fit)) {
    fit <- tryCatch(
      lmerTest::lmer(formula_simple, data = data, REML = TRUE),
      error = function(e) lm(lme4::nobars(formula_simple), data = data)
    )
  }
  if (inherits(fit, "merMod") && lme4::isSingular(fit)) {
    message("[LMM NOTE] Singular fit detected in ", label, ": ", paste(deparse(formula(fit)), collapse = " "))
  }
  fit
}

#' Run Full Mixed-Effects Analysis for Experiment 1 (Synthetic Benchmark Suite)
analyze_synthetic_mixed_effects <- function(df_synth) {
  dt <- prepare_mixed_model_data(df_synth, ref_method = "BGNLM_Full_Tuned")
  dt[, Seed_ID := paste0(System, "_N", SampleSize, "_eta", Noise, "_s", Seed)]
  dt[, log10_Noise := log10(pmax(as.numeric(Noise), 1e-6))]
  dt[, log10_N := log10(pmax(as.numeric(SampleSize), 10))]
  dt[, One_Minus_FDR := 1.0 - FDR]

  # Check if log10_Noise / log10_N vary in the dataset (smoke test may have 1 noise & 1 N)
  covars <- character(0)
  if (uniqueN(dt$log10_Noise) > 1L) covars <- c(covars, "log10_Noise")
  if (uniqueN(dt$log10_N) > 1L) covars <- c(covars, "log10_N")
  covar_str <- if (length(covars) > 0L) paste("+", paste(covars, collapse = " + ")) else ""

  metrics <- c("F1", "TPR", "FDR", "R2_test_in", "R2_test_ood", "R2_traj")
  lmm_fits <- list()
  tidy_rows <- list()
  dunnett_rows <- list()
  icc_rows <- list()

  for (m_name in metrics) {
    if (!m_name %in% names(dt)) next
    f_full <- as.formula(paste0(m_name, " ~ Method ", covar_str, " + (1 | System) + (1 | System:Method) + (1 | System:Seed_ID)"))
    f_simp <- as.formula(paste0(m_name, " ~ Method ", covar_str, " + (1 | Seed_ID)"))
    fit_m <- safe_fit_lmm(f_full, f_simp, dt, label = paste0("Synth_", m_name))
    lmm_fits[[m_name]] <- fit_m

    td <- as.data.table(broom.mixed::tidy(fit_m, effects = "fixed", conf.int = TRUE))
    td[, Metric := m_name]

    if (inherits(fit_m, "merMod")) {
      icc_rows[[length(icc_rows) + 1L]] <- extract_random_effects_icc(fit_m, model_label = paste0("Synth_", m_name))
    }

    # Dunnett-adjusted treatment vs. control (BGNLM_Full_Tuned) contrasts + simultaneous vs unadjusted 95% CIs
    emm <- tryCatch({
      em_obj <- suppressMessages(emmeans::emmeans(fit_m, specs = "Method", lmer.df = "satterthwaite", lmerTest.limit = 150000))
      contr <- emmeans::contrast(em_obj, method = "trt.vs.ctrl", ref = 1, adjust = "dunnett")
      s_dun <- as.data.table(summary(contr, infer = c(TRUE, TRUE), adjust = "dunnett"))
      s_non <- as.data.table(summary(contr, infer = c(TRUE, FALSE), adjust = "none"))
      if (all(c("lower.CL", "upper.CL") %in% names(s_dun))) {
        setnames(s_dun, c("lower.CL", "upper.CL"), c("dunnett.low", "dunnett.high"))
      }
      if (all(c("lower.CL", "upper.CL") %in% names(s_non)) && nrow(s_non) == nrow(s_dun)) {
        s_dun[, unadj.low := s_non$lower.CL]
        s_dun[, unadj.high := s_non$upper.CL]
      }
      s_dun
    }, error = function(e) NULL)
    td <- attach_dunnett_pvals(td, emm)
    tidy_rows[[length(tidy_rows) + 1L]] <- td
    if (!is.null(emm)) {
      emm[, Metric := m_name]
      dunnett_rows[[length(dunnett_rows) + 1L]] <- emm
    }
  }

  # Regime Moderation Interaction Model + Within-Regime Dunnett Contrasts: F1 ~ Method * SystemGroup
  fit_regime <- NULL
  tidy_regime <- data.table()
  regime_dunnett <- data.table()
  if ("SystemGroup" %in% names(dt) && uniqueN(dt$SystemGroup) > 1L) {
    dt[, SystemGroup := factor(SystemGroup)]
    f_reg_full <- as.formula(paste0("F1 ~ Method * SystemGroup ", covar_str, " + (1 | System) + (1 | System:Method) + (1 | System:Seed_ID)"))
    f_reg_simp <- as.formula(paste0("F1 ~ Method * SystemGroup ", covar_str, " + (1 | Seed_ID)"))
    fit_regime <- safe_fit_lmm(f_reg_full, f_reg_simp, dt, label = "Synth_Regime")
    tidy_regime <- as.data.table(broom.mixed::tidy(fit_regime, effects = "fixed", conf.int = TRUE))
    tidy_regime[, Metric := "F1_Regime"]
    tidy_regime[, p.dunnett := p.adjust(p.value, method = "holm")]

    regime_dunnett <- tryCatch({
      em_reg <- suppressMessages(emmeans::emmeans(
        fit_regime, specs = ~ Method | SystemGroup,
        lmer.df = "satterthwaite", lmerTest.limit = 150000
      ))
      contr_reg <- emmeans::contrast(em_reg, method = "trt.vs.ctrl", ref = 1, adjust = "dunnett")
      as.data.table(summary(contr_reg, infer = c(TRUE, TRUE), adjust = "dunnett"))
    }, error = function(e) data.table())
  }

  # Binomial GLMM for Exact Model Recovery (I_exact in {0, 1}) with block RE (1 | System:Seed_ID)
  fit_glmm <- NULL
  tidy_glmm <- data.table()
  if ("Exact_Recovery" %in% names(dt) && uniqueN(dt$Exact_Recovery) > 1L) {
    glmm_ctrl <- glmmTMB::glmmTMBControl(parallel = min(4L, max(1L, parallel::detectCores(logical = FALSE))))
    f_glmm_full <- as.formula(paste0("Exact_Recovery ~ Method ", covar_str, " + (1 | System) + (1 | System:Method) + (1 | System:Seed_ID)"))
    f_glmm_red <- as.formula(paste0("Exact_Recovery ~ Method ", covar_str, " + (1 | System) + (1 | System:Seed_ID)"))
    fit_glmm <- tryCatch(
      glmmTMB::glmmTMB(f_glmm_full, data = dt, family = binomial(link = "logit"), control = glmm_ctrl),
      error = function(e) {
        tryCatch(
          glmmTMB::glmmTMB(f_glmm_red, data = dt, family = binomial(link = "logit"), control = glmm_ctrl),
          error = function(e2) {
            tryCatch(
              glmmTMB::glmmTMB(as.formula(paste0("Exact_Recovery ~ Method ", covar_str, " + (1 | System) + (1 | System:Method)")), data = dt, family = binomial(link = "logit"), control = glmm_ctrl),
              error = function(e3) NULL
            )
          }
        )
      }
    )
    if (!is.null(fit_glmm)) {
      tidy_glmm <- tryCatch(
        as.data.table(broom.mixed::tidy(fit_glmm, effects = "fixed", conf.int = TRUE)),
        error = function(e) data.table()
      )
      if (nrow(tidy_glmm) > 0L) {
        tidy_glmm[, Metric := "Exact_Recovery_Logit"]
        tidy_glmm[, p.dunnett := ifelse(startsWith(term, "Method"), p.adjust(p.value, method = "holm"), NA_real_)]
      }
    }
  }

  # Ordered Beta GLMM sensitivity check (Kubinec 2023) on [0,1]-bounded F1, FDR, R2_traj
  ordbeta_rows <- list()
  lmm_dun_dt <- rbindlist(dunnett_rows, fill = TRUE)
  ob_ctrl <- glmmTMB::glmmTMBControl(parallel = min(4L, max(1L, parallel::detectCores(logical = FALSE))))
  for (ob_m in c("F1", "FDR", "R2_traj")) {
    if (!ob_m %in% names(dt)) next
    dt_ob <- dt[is.finite(get(ob_m))]
    dt_ob[, y_ob := pmin(1.0, pmax(0.0, as.numeric(get(ob_m))))]
    # Fit cross-system random-interaction Ordered Beta GLMM (132 System:Method levels keep Laplace exact & fast)
    f_ob <- as.formula(paste0("y_ob ~ Method ", covar_str, " + (1 | System) + (1 | System:Method)"))
    fit_ob <- tryCatch(
      glmmTMB::glmmTMB(f_ob, data = dt_ob, family = glmmTMB::ordbeta(), control = ob_ctrl),
      error = function(e) {
        tryCatch(glmmTMB::glmmTMB(as.formula(paste0("y_ob ~ Method ", covar_str, " + (1 | System)")), data = dt_ob, family = glmmTMB::ordbeta(), control = ob_ctrl), error = function(e2) NULL)
      }
    )
    if (!is.null(fit_ob)) {
      em_ob <- tryCatch({
        em_o <- suppressMessages(emmeans::emmeans(fit_ob, specs = "Method"))
        contr_o <- emmeans::contrast(em_o, method = "trt.vs.ctrl", ref = 1, adjust = "dunnett")
        dt_o <- as.data.table(summary(contr_o, infer = c(TRUE, TRUE), adjust = "dunnett"))
        dt_o[, Metric := ob_m]
        dt_o
      }, error = function(e) NULL)
      if (!is.null(em_ob)) ordbeta_rows[[length(ordbeta_rows) + 1L]] <- em_ob
    }
  }
  ordbeta_dt <- rbindlist(ordbeta_rows, fill = TRUE)

  list(
    lmm_fits = lmm_fits,
    fit_regime = fit_regime,
    fit_glmm = fit_glmm,
    fixed_effects = rbindlist(tidy_rows, fill = TRUE),
    regime_effects = tidy_regime,
    regime_dunnett = regime_dunnett,
    glmm_effects = tidy_glmm,
    dunnett_contrasts = lmm_dun_dt,
    ordbeta_contrasts = ordbeta_dt,
    icc_table = rbindlist(icc_rows, fill = TRUE)
  )
}

#' Run Full Mixed-Effects Analysis for Experiment 2 (Real-World Empirical Datasets)
analyze_real_mixed_effects <- function(df_real) {
  dt <- prepare_mixed_model_data(df_real, ref_method = "BGNLM_Full_Tuned")
  dt[, Fold_UID := paste0(Dataset, "_", DiffMethod, "_fold", Fold_ID)]

  has_multi_diff <- ("DiffMethod" %in% names(dt)) && (uniqueN(dt$DiffMethod) > 1L)
  diff_str <- if (has_multi_diff) "+ DiffMethod" else ""

  metrics <- c("R2_traj", "NRMSE_traj", "R2_test_ood", "F1")
  lmm_fits <- list()
  tidy_rows <- list()
  dunnett_rows <- list()
  icc_rows <- list()

  for (m_name in metrics) {
    if (!m_name %in% names(dt)) next
    f_full <- as.formula(paste0(m_name, " ~ Method ", diff_str, " + (1 | Dataset) + (1 | Dataset:Method) + (1 | Dataset:Fold_UID)"))
    f_simp <- as.formula(paste0(m_name, " ~ Method ", diff_str, " + (1 | Fold_UID)"))
    fit_m <- safe_fit_lmm(f_full, f_simp, dt, label = paste0("Real_", m_name))
    lmm_fits[[m_name]] <- fit_m

    td <- as.data.table(broom.mixed::tidy(fit_m, effects = "fixed", conf.int = TRUE))
    td[, Metric := m_name]

    if (inherits(fit_m, "merMod")) {
      icc_rows[[length(icc_rows) + 1L]] <- extract_random_effects_icc(fit_m, model_label = paste0("Real_", m_name))
    }

    emm <- tryCatch({
      em_obj <- suppressMessages(emmeans::emmeans(fit_m, specs = "Method", lmer.df = "satterthwaite", lmerTest.limit = 10000))
      contr <- emmeans::contrast(em_obj, method = "trt.vs.ctrl", ref = 1, adjust = "dunnett")
      s_dun <- as.data.table(summary(contr, infer = c(TRUE, TRUE), adjust = "dunnett"))
      s_non <- as.data.table(summary(contr, infer = c(TRUE, FALSE), adjust = "none"))
      if (all(c("lower.CL", "upper.CL") %in% names(s_dun))) {
        setnames(s_dun, c("lower.CL", "upper.CL"), c("dunnett.low", "dunnett.high"))
      }
      if (all(c("lower.CL", "upper.CL") %in% names(s_non)) && nrow(s_non) == nrow(s_dun)) {
        s_dun[, unadj.low := s_non$lower.CL]
        s_dun[, unadj.high := s_non$upper.CL]
      }
      s_dun
    }, error = function(e) NULL)
    td <- attach_dunnett_pvals(td, emm)
    tidy_rows[[length(tidy_rows) + 1L]] <- td
    if (!is.null(emm)) {
      emm[, Metric := m_name]
      dunnett_rows[[length(dunnett_rows) + 1L]] <- emm
    }
  }

  list(
    lmm_fits = lmm_fits,
    fixed_effects = rbindlist(tidy_rows, fill = TRUE),
    dunnett_contrasts = rbindlist(dunnett_rows, fill = TRUE),
    icc_table = rbindlist(icc_rows, fill = TRUE)
  )
}



=== FILE: R/09_plotting_tables.R ===
# ==============================================================================
# R/09_plotting_tables.R
# ICML Publication-Ready ggplot2 Figures & LaTeX Booktabs Tables (Okabe-Ito)
# ==============================================================================

suppressPackageStartupMessages({
  library(ggplot2)
  library(data.table)
})

#' Format p-value for LaTeX scientific tables
format_pval_latex <- function(p) {
  ifelse(is.na(p), "--",
    ifelse(p < 0.0001, "< 10^{-4}",
      sprintf("%.4f", p)))
}

#' Map raw method strings to human-readable academic labels
human_method_label <- function(m_str) {
  m_str <- as.character(m_str)
  map <- c(
    "BGNLM_Full_Tuned"    = "BGNLM (Tuned)",
    "BGLM_Oracle_Basis"   = "BGLM (Oracle)",
    "BGNLM_Hybrid_Poly"   = "BGNLM-Hybrid",
    "BGLM_Poly_Basis"     = "BGLM (Poly)",
    "BGLM_Overcomplete"   = "BGLM (Overcomplete)",
    "BGNLM_IWSM_Untuned"  = "BGNLM (Untuned IWSM)",
    "SINDy_STLSQ_Oracle"  = "STLSQ (Oracle)",
    "SINDy_STLSQ_Poly"    = "STLSQ (Poly)",
    "UQ_SINDy_Oracle"     = "Bag-STLSQ (Oracle)",
    "Bag_STLSQ_Oracle"    = "Bag-STLSQ (Oracle)",
    "UQ_SINDy_Poly"       = "Bag-STLSQ (Poly)",
    "Bag_STLSQ_Poly"      = "Bag-STLSQ (Poly)",
    "SINDy_PI_Rational"   = "SINDy-RatPoly2",
    "SINDy_RatPoly2"      = "SINDy-RatPoly2",
    "SR_Operon"           = "SR Pool (MDL)",
    "SR_Pool_MDL"         = "SR Pool (MDL)",
    "SR_PySR"             = "SR Pool (Pareto)",
    "SR_Pool_Pareto"      = "SR Pool (Pareto)",
    "UQ_SINDy_Bayes_Poly" = "BAS-SINDy (Poly)",
    "UQ_SINDy_Bayes_Oracle" = "BAS-SINDy (Oracle)",
    "WSINDy_Poly"         = "WSINDy (Poly)",
    "WSINDy_Oracle"       = "WSINDy (Oracle)"
  )
  out <- ifelse(m_str %in% names(map), map[m_str], m_str)
  unname(out)
}

#' Assign Method Family for Okabe-Ito colorblind-safe palettes
assign_method_family <- function(m_str) {
  m_str <- as.character(m_str)
  ifelse(grepl("^BGNLM", m_str), "BGNLM (Basis-Free)",
    ifelse(grepl("^BGLM", m_str), "Predefined BGLM",
      ifelse(grepl("SINDy|STLSQ|WSINDy|BAS", m_str), "SINDy / UQ-SINDy",
        "Symbolic Regression")))
}

okabe_ito_family_pal <- c(
  "BGNLM (Basis-Free)"  = "#0072B2",
  "Predefined BGLM"     = "#009E73",
  "SINDy / UQ-SINDy"    = "#D55E00",
  "Symbolic Regression" = "#9467BD"
)

#' Clean term label for publication LaTeX tables
clean_term_for_latex <- function(term_str) {
  s <- gsub("^Method", "", as.character(term_str))
  s <- gsub("SystemGroupGroup_", "Group_", s, fixed = TRUE)
  s <- gsub("DiffMethodtvregdiff", "Diff: TVRegDiff", s, fixed = TRUE)
  if (exists("relabel_method_str", mode = "function")) {
    s <- relabel_method_str(s)
  }
  s <- gsub("_", "\\\\_", s)
  s
}

#' Clean metric name for LaTeX tables
clean_metric_latex <- function(m_str) {
  map <- c(
    "F1" = "$F_1$",
    "FDR" = "FDR",
    "TPR" = "TPR",
    "R2_test_ood" = "$R^2_{\\text{OOD}}$",
    "R2_test_in" = "$R^2_{\\text{in}}$",
    "R2_traj" = "$R^2_{\\text{traj}}$",
    "NRMSE_traj" = "$\\text{NRMSE}_{\\text{traj}}$",
    "Exact_Recovery_Logit" = "$I_{\\text{exact}}$ (Logit)",
    "F1_Regime" = "$F_1$ Interaction",
    "Group_A_Polynomial" = "Group A ($F_1$)",
    "Group_B_NonPolynomial" = "Group B ($F_1$)",
    "Group_C_HighDim" = "Group C ($F_1$)"
  )
  out <- ifelse(m_str %in% names(map), map[m_str], gsub("_", "\\\\_", as.character(m_str)))
  unname(out)
}

#' Export Fixed-Effects Mixed-Model Contrasts to Clean Booktabs LaTeX Table
export_mixed_effects_latex_table <- function(fixed_dt, out_path, caption_str, label_str) {
  dir.create(dirname(out_path), recursive = TRUE, showWarnings = FALSE)
  dt <- copy(as.data.table(fixed_dt))
  if (!"df" %in% names(dt)) dt[, df := NA_real_]
  if (!"p.value" %in% names(dt)) dt[, p.value := NA_real_]
  if (!"p.dunnett" %in% names(dt)) dt[, p.dunnett := NA_real_]
  if (!"conf.low" %in% names(dt)) dt[, conf.low := estimate - 1.96 * std.error]
  if (!"conf.high" %in% names(dt)) dt[, conf.high := estimate + 1.96 * std.error]

  has_dunnett <- any(!is.na(dt$p.dunnett))
  is_z_model <- all(is.na(dt$df) | !is.finite(dt$df)) || any(grepl("Logit|glmm|ordbeta", dt$Metric, ignore.case = TRUE))
  stat_hdr <- if (is_z_model) "$z$" else "$t$"
  col_spec <- if (has_dunnett) "llccccccc" else "llcccccc"
  hdr_extra <- if (has_dunnett) " & $p_{\\text{adj}}$" else ""

  lines <- c(
    "\\begin{table*}[t]",
    "\\centering",
    paste0("\\caption{", caption_str, "}"),
    paste0("\\label{", label_str, "}"),
    "\\scriptsize",
    "\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.5pt}",
    "\\renewcommand{\\arraystretch}{0.88}",
    paste0("\\begin{tabular}{", col_spec, "}"),
    "\\toprule",
    paste0("Metric & Term / Contrast (vs.\\ \\texttt{BGNLM\\_Full\\_Tuned}) & $\\hat{\\beta}$ & SE & $df$ & ", stat_hdr, " & $95\\%$ CI & $p$-value", hdr_extra, " \\\\"),
    "\\midrule"
  )

  prev_m <- ""
  for (i in seq_len(nrow(dt))) {
    r <- dt[i]
    cur_m <- as.character(r$Metric)
    if (i > 1L && cur_m != prev_m) {
      lines <- c(lines, "\\midrule")
    }
    prev_m <- cur_m

    term_clean <- clean_term_for_latex(r$term)
    metric_clean <- clean_metric_latex(cur_m)
    df_str <- if (is.na(r$df) || !is.finite(r$df)) "--" else sprintf("%.1f", r$df)
    stat_str <- if (is.na(r$statistic)) "--" else sprintf("%.2f", r$statistic)
    ci_str <- sprintf("[%.3f, %.3f]", r$conf.low, r$conf.high)
    p_str <- paste0("$", format_pval_latex(r$p.value), "$")
    padj_col <- if (has_dunnett) {
      if (is.na(r$p.dunnett)) " & --" else paste0(" & $", format_pval_latex(r$p.dunnett), "$")
    } else ""

    lines <- c(lines, sprintf(
      "%s & \\texttt{%s} & %.3f & %.3f & %s & %s & %s & %s%s \\\\",
      metric_clean, term_clean, r$estimate, r$std.error, df_str, stat_str, ci_str, p_str, padj_col
    ))
  }

  lines <- c(lines, "\\bottomrule", "\\end{tabular}", "\\end{table*}")
  writeLines(lines, out_path)
  invisible(out_path)
}

#' Export ICC Variance Decomposition Table to LaTeX (Booktabs, no vertical rules)
export_icc_latex_table <- function(icc_synth, icc_real, out_path) {
  dir.create(dirname(out_path), recursive = TRUE, showWarnings = FALSE)
  dt <- rbindlist(list(icc_synth, icc_real), fill = TRUE)
  has_sing <- "Singular" %in% names(dt)
  col_spec <- if (has_sing) "llcccc" else "llccc"
  sing_hdr <- if (has_sing) " & Singular" else ""
  lines <- c(
    "\\begin{table}[t]",
    "\\centering",
    "\\caption{Complete Random-Effects Variance Decomposition and Intraclass Correlation Coefficients (ICC).}",
    "\\label{tab:mixed_effects_icc}",
    "\\scriptsize",
    "\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.5pt}",
    paste0("\\begin{tabular}{", col_spec, "}"),
    "\\toprule",
    paste0("Model & Random Effect Group & $\\hat{\\sigma}^2$ & $\\hat{\\sigma}$ & ICC", sing_hdr, " \\\\"),
    "\\midrule"
  )
  prev_mod <- ""
  for (i in seq_len(nrow(dt))) {
    r <- dt[i]
    if (i > 1L && r$Model != prev_mod) lines <- c(lines, "\\midrule")
    prev_mod <- r$Model
    m_clean <- gsub("_", "\\\\_", r$Model)
    g_clean <- gsub("_", "\\\\_", r$Group)
    s_col <- if (has_sing) paste0(" & ", r$Singular) else ""
    lines <- c(lines, sprintf(
      "%s & \\texttt{%s} & %.4f & %.4f & %.3f%s \\\\",
      m_clean, g_clean, r$Variance, r$StdDev, r$ICC, s_col
    ))
  }
  lines <- c(lines, "\\bottomrule", "\\end{tabular}", "\\end{table}")
  writeLines(lines, out_path)
  invisible(out_path)
}

#' Generate Publication-Quality Mixed-Effects Forest Plot (Dual Unadjusted & Simultaneous Dunnett 95% CIs)
plot_mixed_effects_forest <- function(fixed_dt, out_pdf, title_str = NULL, dunnett_dt = NULL) {
  dir.create(dirname(out_pdf), recursive = TRUE, showWarnings = FALSE)
  dt <- copy(as.data.table(fixed_dt))
  dt <- dt[grepl("^Method", term)]
  if (nrow(dt) == 0L) return(invisible(NULL))

  dt[, Raw_Method := gsub("^Method", "", term)]
  dt[, Method_Label := human_method_label(Raw_Method)]
  dt[, Family := factor(assign_method_family(Raw_Method), levels = names(okabe_ito_family_pal))]

  if (!"conf.low" %in% names(dt)) dt[, conf.low := estimate - 1.96 * std.error]
  if (!"conf.high" %in% names(dt)) dt[, conf.high := estimate + 1.96 * std.error]

  dt[, dun.low := conf.low]
  dt[, dun.high := conf.high]
  if (!is.null(dunnett_dt) && nrow(dunnett_dt) > 0L && all(c("dunnett.low", "dunnett.high") %in% names(dunnett_dt))) {
    dd <- copy(as.data.table(dunnett_dt))
    dd[, Raw_Method := sub(" - .*", "", contrast)]
    for (i in seq_len(nrow(dt))) {
      hit <- dd[Metric == dt$Metric[i] & Raw_Method == dt$Raw_Method[i]]
      if (nrow(hit) >= 1L) {
        dt[i, dun.low := hit$dunnett.low[1L]]
        dt[i, dun.high := hit$dunnett.high[1L]]
        if ("unadj.low" %in% names(hit) && is.finite(hit$unadj.low[1L])) {
          dt[i, conf.low := hit$unadj.low[1L]]
          dt[i, conf.high := hit$unadj.high[1L]]
        }
      }
    }
  }

  metric_map <- c(
    "F1" = "Structural F1 Contrast",
    "FDR" = "False Discovery Rate Contrast",
    "R2_test_ood" = "Derivative R2 (OOD) Contrast",
    "R2_traj" = "Trajectory Rollout R2 Contrast",
    "NRMSE_traj" = "Rollout NRMSE Contrast"
  )
  ordered_metrics <- unique(c(intersect(names(metric_map), unique(dt$Metric)),
                              setdiff(unique(dt$Metric), names(metric_map))))
  panel_labels <- setNames(
    paste0("(", letters[seq_along(ordered_metrics)], ") ",
           ifelse(ordered_metrics %in% names(metric_map), metric_map[ordered_metrics], ordered_metrics)),
    ordered_metrics
  )
  dt[, Metric_Panel := factor(panel_labels[Metric], levels = unname(panel_labels))]

  # Order methods consistently by F1 (or first metric) contrast
  ord_df <- dt[Metric == dt$Metric[1], .(ord_est = mean(estimate)), by = Method_Label]
  dt <- merge(dt, ord_df, by = "Method_Label", all.x = TRUE)
  dt[, Method_Label := reorder(Method_Label, ord_est)]

  n_panels <- uniqueN(dt$Metric_Panel)
  nrow_wrap <- if (n_panels == 4L) 2L else 1L
  fig_w <- 7.4
  fig_h <- if (n_panels == 4L) 4.6 else 3.0

  p <- ggplot(dt, aes(x = estimate, y = Method_Label, color = Family)) +
    geom_vline(xintercept = 0.0, linetype = "dashed", color = "grey30", linewidth = 0.55) +
    geom_errorbar(aes(xmin = dun.low, xmax = dun.high), orientation = "y", width = 0.30, linewidth = 0.50, alpha = 0.85) +
    geom_segment(aes(x = conf.low, xend = conf.high, y = Method_Label, yend = Method_Label), linewidth = 1.25) +
    geom_point(size = 2.0) +
    scale_color_manual(values = okabe_ito_family_pal, drop = TRUE) +
    facet_wrap(~ Metric_Panel, nrow = nrow_wrap, scales = "free_x") +
    labs(
      x = "Mixed-Effects Contrast vs. BGNLM (Tuned) [Inner: Unadj. 95% CI, Outer: Simultaneous Dunnett 95% CI]",
      y = NULL,
      color = "Method Family"
    ) +
    theme_bw(base_size = 8.5) +
    theme(
      legend.position = "bottom",
      legend.title = element_text(size = 7.8, face = "bold"),
      legend.text = element_text(size = 7.0),
      strip.background = element_rect(fill = "#eef2f6", color = "grey75"),
      strip.text = element_text(face = "bold", size = 8),
      panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
      axis.text.y = element_text(size = 7.5),
      axis.title.x = element_text(size = 7.6),
      plot.margin = margin(5, 10, 5, 5)
    ) +
    guides(color = guide_legend(nrow = 1))

  ggsave(out_pdf, p, width = fig_w, height = fig_h, bg = "white")
  ggsave(sub("\\.pdf$", ".png", out_pdf), p, width = fig_w, height = fig_h, dpi = 220, bg = "white")
  paper_fig <- file.path("paper_icml/figures", basename(out_pdf))
  if (dir.exists("paper_icml/figures")) ggsave(paper_fig, p, width = fig_w, height = fig_h, bg = "white")
  invisible(p)
}

#' Generate 4-Panel Full-Width Benchmark Overview (F1, FDR, R2_OOD, R2_traj across Groups A/B/C)
plot_benchmark_summary <- function(df_synth, out_pdf = "results/figures/fig_synth_overview.pdf") {
  dir.create(dirname(out_pdf), recursive = TRUE, showWarnings = FALSE)
  dt <- copy(as.data.table(df_synth))
  if (nrow(dt) == 0L) return(invisible(NULL))

  dt[, Method_Label := human_method_label(Method)]
  group_map <- c(
    "Group_A_Polynomial"    = "Group A: Poly",
    "Group_B_NonPolynomial" = "Group B: Non-Poly",
    "Group_C_HighDim"       = "Group C: 5D Lorenz-96"
  )
  dt[, Group_Short := factor(group_map[SystemGroup], levels = unname(group_map))]

  # Aggregate across all reps per Method x Group
  long_dt <- rbindlist(list(
    dt[, .(Val = mean(F1, na.rm = TRUE), SE = sd(F1, na.rm = TRUE) / sqrt(sum(!is.na(F1))), Panel = "(a) Structural F1 (higher better)"), by = .(Method_Label, Group_Short)],
    dt[, .(Val = mean(FDR, na.rm = TRUE), SE = sd(FDR, na.rm = TRUE) / sqrt(sum(!is.na(FDR))), Panel = "(b) False Discovery Rate (lower better)"), by = .(Method_Label, Group_Short)],
    dt[, .(Val = mean(R2_test_ood, na.rm = TRUE), SE = sd(R2_test_ood, na.rm = TRUE) / sqrt(sum(!is.na(R2_test_ood))), Panel = "(c) Derivative R2 OOD (higher better)"), by = .(Method_Label, Group_Short)],
    dt[, .(Val = mean(R2_traj, na.rm = TRUE), SE = sd(R2_traj, na.rm = TRUE) / sqrt(sum(!is.na(R2_traj))), Panel = "(d) Trajectory Rollout R2 (higher better)"), by = .(Method_Label, Group_Short)]
  ))
  long_dt[, Panel := factor(Panel, levels = c(
    "(a) Structural F1 (higher better)",
    "(b) False Discovery Rate (lower better)",
    "(c) Derivative R2 OOD (higher better)",
    "(d) Trajectory Rollout R2 (higher better)"
  ))]

  ord_m <- long_dt[Panel == "(a) Structural F1 (higher better)", .(m = mean(Val, na.rm = TRUE)), by = Method_Label]
  long_dt <- merge(long_dt, ord_m, by = "Method_Label", all.x = TRUE)
  long_dt[, Method_Label := reorder(Method_Label, m)]

  grp_pal <- c(
    "Group A: Poly" = "#0072B2",
    "Group B: Non-Poly" = "#009E73",
    "Group C: 5D Lorenz-96" = "#D55E00"
  )

  p <- ggplot(long_dt, aes(x = Method_Label, y = Val, fill = Group_Short)) +
    geom_col(position = position_dodge(width = 0.78), width = 0.72) +
    geom_errorbar(
      aes(ymin = pmax(0, Val - 1.96 * SE), ymax = pmin(1, Val + 1.96 * SE)),
      position = position_dodge(width = 0.78), width = 0.25, linewidth = 0.35
    ) +
    coord_flip(ylim = c(0, 1.02)) +
    scale_fill_manual(values = grp_pal) +
    facet_wrap(~ Panel, nrow = 2, ncol = 2) +
    labs(x = NULL, y = "Mean Score (+/- 1.96 SE)", fill = "Dynamical Regime") +
    theme_bw(base_size = 8.5) +
    theme(
      legend.position = "bottom",
      legend.title = element_text(size = 8, face = "bold"),
      legend.text = element_text(size = 7.5),
      strip.background = element_rect(fill = "#eef2f6", color = "grey75"),
      strip.text = element_text(face = "bold", size = 8),
      panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),
      axis.text.y = element_text(size = 7.2)
    )

  ggsave(out_pdf, p, width = 6.75, height = 4.6, bg = "white")
  ggsave(sub("\\.pdf$", ".png", out_pdf), p, width = 6.75, height = 4.6, dpi = 220, bg = "white")
  if (dir.exists("paper_icml/figures")) ggsave(file.path("paper_icml/figures", basename(out_pdf)), p, width = 6.75, height = 4.6, bg = "white")
  invisible(p)
}

#' Registry of the 5 Canonical Real-World Empirical Datasets
get_real_datasets_registry <- function() {
  list(
    real01_lynx_hare = list(
      dataset_id = 1L,
      id = "real01_lynx_hare",
      name = "Hudson Bay Lynx-Hare Pelts",
      domain = "Ecology",
      file = "data/real_world/lynx_hare_hudson_bay.csv",
      dim = 2L,
      state_names = c("x1", "x2"),
      input_names = character(0),
      feature_names = c("x1", "x2"),
      ground_truth = list(
        c("x1", "x1*x2"),
        c("x2", "x1*x2")
      ),
      oracle_extra_basis = character(0)
    ),

    real02_paramecium_didinium = list(
      dataset_id = 2L,
      id = "real02_paramecium_didinium",
      name = "Gause-Veilleux Paramecium-Didinium",
      domain = "Experimental Microcosm",
      file = "data/real_world/gause_paramecium_didinium.csv",
      dim = 2L,
      state_names = c("x1", "x2"),
      input_names = character(0),
      feature_names = c("x1", "x2"),
      ground_truth = list(
        c("x1", "x1^2", "x1*x2/(1+x1)"),
        c("x2", "x1*x2/(1+x1)")
      ),
      oracle_extra_basis = c("1/(1+x1)", "x1/(1+x1)", "x1*x2/(1+x1)")
    ),

    real03_schmidt_pendulum = list(
      dataset_id = 3L,
      id = "real03_schmidt_pendulum",
      name = "Schmidt-Lipson Physical Pendulum",
      domain = "Lab Mechanics",
      file = "data/real_world/schmidt_real_pendulum.csv",
      dim = 2L,
      state_names = c("x1", "x2"),
      input_names = character(0),
      feature_names = c("x1", "x2"),
      ground_truth = list(
        c("x2"),
        c("x2", "sin(x1)")
      ),
      oracle_extra_basis = c("sin(x1)", "cos(x1)")
    ),

    real04_cascaded_tanks = list(
      dataset_id = 4L,
      id = "real04_cascaded_tanks",
      name = "Cascaded Water Tanks Benchmark",
      domain = "Nonlinear System Identification",
      file = "data/real_world/cascaded_tanks_benchmark.csv",
      dim = 2L,
      state_names = c("x1", "x2"),
      input_names = c("x3"),
      feature_names = c("x1", "x2", "x3"),
      ground_truth = list(
        c("sqrt(x1)", "x3"),
        c("sqrt(x1)", "sqrt(x2)")
      ),
      oracle_extra_basis = c("sqrt(x1)", "sqrt(x2)")
    ),

    real05_yeast_glycolysis = list(
      dataset_id = 5L,
      id = "real05_yeast_glycolysis",
      name = "Yeast Glycolytic NADH Oscillations",
      domain = "Biochemistry",
      file = "data/real_world/yeast_glycolysis_nadh.csv",
      dim = 2L,
      state_names = c("x1", "x2"),
      input_names = character(0),
      feature_names = c("x1", "x2"),
      ground_truth = list(
        c("x1", "x1*x2^2/(1+x2^2)"),
        c("x1", "x2", "x1*x2^2/(1+x2^2)")
      ),
      oracle_extra_basis = c("1/(1+x2^2)", "x2^2/(1+x2^2)", "x1*x2^2/(1+x2^2)")
    )
  )
}

#' Generate a Real-World Dataset Task Split (Rolling Temporal Split + 85% Subsampling)
generate_real_task_data <- function(
  dataset_id,
  diff_method = c("sgolay_spline", "tvregdiff"),
  fold_id = 1L,
  seed = 50101L
) {
  diff_method <- match.arg(diff_method)
  reg <- get_real_datasets_registry()
  if (!dataset_id %in% names(reg)) {
    stop("Unknown real dataset_id: ", dataset_id)
  }
  ds <- reg[[dataset_id]]
  if (!file.exists(ds$file)) {
    source("scripts/download_real_data.R")
  }
  df <- read.csv(ds$file, stringsAsFactors = FALSE)
  times <- as.numeric(df$time)
  X_states <- as.matrix(df[, ds$state_names, drop = FALSE])
  X_features <- as.matrix(df[, ds$feature_names, drop = FALSE])

  n_total <- nrow(X_features)
  # Rolling expanding-window cutoff around 70% train (varies between 65% and 75% across folds)
  set.seed(as.integer(seed))
  frac_train <- 0.65 + 0.10 * ((fold_id - 1L) %% 10L) / 9.0
  n_train_window <- max(20L, floor(frac_train * n_total))
  idx_train_pool <- seq_len(n_train_window)
  idx_ood <- seq(from = n_train_window + 1L, to = n_total)

  # ZERO PRE-SPLIT LEAKAGE: differentiate training window and OOD window strictly separately
  diff_fn <- if (diff_method == "sgolay_spline") estimate_derivatives_sgolay_spline else estimate_derivatives_tvregdiff
  Y_train_window <- diff_fn(X_states[idx_train_pool, , drop = FALSE], times[idx_train_pool])
  Y_ood_window <- diff_fn(X_states[idx_ood, , drop = FALSE], times[idx_ood])

  # 85% subsample of training window for structural stability; remaining 15% serves as in-sample validation
  n_sub <- max(15L, floor(0.85 * length(idx_train_pool)))
  idx_train_local <- sort(sample(seq_along(idx_train_pool), size = n_sub, replace = FALSE))
  idx_in_local <- setdiff(seq_along(idx_train_pool), idx_train_local)
  if (length(idx_in_local) < 3L) {
    idx_in_local <- idx_train_local
  }
  idx_train <- idx_train_pool[idx_train_local]
  idx_in <- idx_train_pool[idx_in_local]

  # Rollout horizon on held-out OOD test window
  rollout_idx <- idx_ood[seq_len(min(length(idx_ood), 100L))]
  rollout_times <- times[rollout_idx] - times[rollout_idx[1]]

  list(
    system = list(
      sys_id = ds$dataset_id,
      id = ds$id,
      name = ds$name,
      group = ds$domain,
      dim = ds$dim,
      state_names = ds$state_names,
      feature_names = ds$feature_names,
      input_names = ds$input_names,
      ground_truth = ds$ground_truth,
      oracle_extra_basis = ds$oracle_extra_basis
    ),
    dataset_id = ds$id,
    diff_method = diff_method,
    fold_id = fold_id,
    seed = seed,
    N_train = length(idx_train),
    t_train = times[idx_train],
    t_seq = times[idx_train_pool],
    X_seq = X_features[idx_train_pool, , drop = FALSE],
    X_train = X_features[idx_train, , drop = FALSE],
    Y_train = Y_train_window[idx_train_local, , drop = FALSE],
    Y_train_clean = Y_train_window[idx_train_local, , drop = FALSE],
    X_test_in = X_features[idx_in, , drop = FALSE],
    Y_test_in_clean = Y_train_window[idx_in_local, , drop = FALSE],
    Y_test_in_noisy = Y_train_window[idx_in_local, , drop = FALSE],
    X_test_ood = X_features[idx_ood, , drop = FALSE],
    Y_test_ood_clean = Y_ood_window,
    Y_test_ood_noisy = Y_ood_window,
    rollout = list(
      times = rollout_times,
      X_true = X_states[rollout_idx, , drop = FALSE],
      X_inputs = if (length(ds$input_names) > 0) X_features[rollout_idx, ds$input_names, drop = FALSE] else NULL,
      x0 = X_states[rollout_idx[1], ]
    )
  )
}


=== FILE: README.md ===
# Bayesian Generalized Nonlinear Models Offer Basis-Free SINDy With Model Uncertainty (ICML Benchmark Suite)

This repository contains the modular, checkpointed, seed-deterministic experimental pipeline expanding *"Bayesian Generalized Nonlinear Models Offer Basis Free SINDy With Model Uncertainty"* (`arXiv:2507.06776`) and the `FBMS` R package (`arXiv:2509.00753`) for ICML.

## Core Contributions & Experimental Architecture

1. **Expanded 12-System Synthetic Benchmark Suite (`R/01_ode_simulators.R`, `config/systems_config.yaml`)**:
   - **Group A (Polynomial ODEs)**: Linear-Quadratic Coupled 3D, Lorenz-63, Rössler Attractor, Lotka–Volterra Predator-Prey, Van der Pol Oscillator, Duffing Oscillator, Brusselator System.
   - **Group B (Non-Polynomial / Transcendental / Rational ODEs)**: Rössler–Lorenz Hybrid 3D, Damped Nonlinear Pendulum, Thomas' Cyclically Symmetric Attractor, Michaelis–Menten / Goodwin Enzymatic Oscillator.
   - **Group C (Higher-Dimensional Coupled ODEs)**: 5D Coupled Lorenz-96 System.
   - Evaluated across sample sizes $N \in \{100, 250, 500, 1000, 2500\}$, standardized derivative noise levels $\eta \in \{0.001, 0.01, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 1.0\}$, state noise with Savitzky–Golay / TVRegDiff differentiation, and $R = 20$ deterministic replications per condition.

2. **Real-World Empirical Datasets Suite (`R/10_real_data_experiments.R`, `scripts/download_real_data.R`)**:
   - **Hudson Bay Lynx–Hare Pelts** (Ecology),
   - **Gause–Veilleux Paramecium–Didinium Laboratory Microcosm** (Experimental Protozoan Ecology),
   - **Schmidt & Lipson Physical Nonlinear Pendulum** (Lab Mechanics),
   - **Cascaded Water Tanks Benchmark** (Nonlinear System Identification),
   - **Yeast Glycolytic NADH Oscillations** (Biochemistry).

3. **13 Competing Method Configurations (`R/03_fbms_wrappers.R`, `R/04_sindy_baselines.R`, `R/04b_sr_baselines.R`)**:
   - **Proposed Basis-Free BGNLM & Ablations (`FBMS`)**: `BGNLM_Full_Tuned` (`gmjmcmc.parallel`), `BGNLM_IWSM_Untuned`, `BGNLM_Hybrid_Poly`.
   - **Predefined-Basis BGLM (`FBMS`)**: `BGLM_Oracle_Basis` (`mjmcmc.parallel`), `BGLM_Poly_Basis`, `BGLM_Overcomplete` ($q > 60$ candidate columns).
   - **Classical, Ensemble/Bayesian, & Implicit SINDy**: `SINDy_STLSQ_Oracle`, `SINDy_STLSQ_Poly`, `UQ_SINDy_Oracle`, `UQ_SINDy_Poly`, `SINDy_PI_Rational`.
   - **Symbolic Regression**: `SR_PySR` (`PySRRegressor` / Pareto knee selection), `SR_Operon` (`PyOperon` / Levenberg–Marquardt + MDL selection).

4. **Symbolic Canonicalization, Selection Consistency, & Mixed-Effects Evaluation (`R/05_symbolic_canonicalize.R`, `R/06_metrics_consistency.R`, `R/08_mixed_effects_eval.R`)**:
   - Algebraic AST canonicalization for exact structural comparison ($\text{TPR}$, $\text{FDR}$, $\text{F1}$, $I_{\text{exact}}$, $\text{SHD}$, Operation Count `OC`, Jaccard & Nogueira stability $\hat{\Phi}$, PIP Brier calibration, $95\%$ interval coverage & Winkler score, $R^2_{\text{in}}$, $R^2_{\text{ood}}$, and forward ODE trajectory rollout $R^2_{\text{traj}}, T_{\text{valid}}$).
   - Linear and Generalized Linear Mixed-Effects Models (`lme4`, `lmerTest`, `glmmTMB`, `emmeans`) with `BGNLM_Full_Tuned` as the reference baseline level.

---

## Quick Start & Execution Commands

```bash
# 1. Install R packages & Python virtual environment (.venv)
make install

# 2. Run unit test suite (ODE verification, symbolic canonicalizer, method wrappers)
make test

# 3. Run systematic BGNLM hyperparameter & prior tuning
make tune

# 4. Run local end-to-end smoke test (2 synthetic systems + 2 real datasets x 13 methods)
make smoke

# 5. Generate full ICML manifests & launch on multi-core remote Linux server
Rscript scripts/generate_manifest.R
./scripts/run_remote_server.sh both 16 4

# 6. Or submit SLURM job arrays on HPC cluster
sbatch slurm/submit_tuning_array.sh
sbatch slurm/submit_benchmark_array.sh
sbatch slurm/submit_real_data_array.sh
```

systems:

  sys01_linear_quadratic_3d:
    sys_id: 1
    name: "Linear-Quadratic Coupled 3D"

    dim: 3
    dt: 0.0001
    T_max: 20.0
    state_names: ["x1", "x2", "x3"]
    initial_conditions: [2.0, 0.0, 1.0]

      a: -1.0
      b: 20.0
      c: -20.0
      d: -1.0
      e: -3.0

      - "\\dot{x}_1 = a x_1 + b x_1 x_2"
      - "\\dot{x}_2 = c x_1 + d x_1 x_2"
      - "\\dot{x}_3 = e x_3"
    ground_truth_atoms:
      eq1: ["x1", "x1*x2"]
      eq2: ["x1", "x1*x2"]
      eq3: ["x3"]

\icmltitlerunning{Bayesian Generalized Nonlinear Models for Basis-Free SINDy}

\begin{document}

\twocolumn[
\icmltitle{Bayesian Generalized Nonlinear Models Offer Basis-Free SINDy\\with Calibrated Model Uncertainty}

\icmlsetsymbol{equal}{*}

\begin{icmlauthorlist}
\icmlauthor{Aliaksandr Hubin}{1,2,3}
\icmlauthor{Jon Lachmann}{3}
\end{icmlauthorlist}

\icmlaffiliation{1}{Norwegian University of Life Sciences (NMBU)}
\icmlaffiliation{2}{\O{}stfold University College (HiOF)}
\icmlaffiliation{3}{Department of Mathematics, University of Oslo, Norway}

\icmlcorrespondingauthor{Aliaksandr Hubin}{aliaksandr.hubin@nmbu.no}

\vskip 0.08in
]

\printAffiliationsAndNotice{}

\begin{abstract}
Discovering parsimonious governing differential equations from noisy time-series data requires resolving a structural dilemma. Classical Sparse Identification of Nonlinear Dynamics (SINDy) and its bagged or Bayesian variants rely on predefined candidate libraries: omitting true nonlinearities causes structural misspecification, whereas overcomplete bases trigger severe multicollinearity and estimation failure. Conversely, evolutionary symbolic regression avoids fixed dictionaries but lacks calibrated posterior inclusion probabilities (PIPs) and Bayesian predictive intervals while exhibiting high run-to-run variance. Building upon our preliminary workshop study \citep{hubin2025bgnlm} and \texttt{FBMS} \citep{lachmann2025fbms}, we present a systematically tuned, identifiability-preserving Bayesian Generalized Nonlinear Model (BGNLM) framework for basis-free SINDy. Our approach integrates unknown-variance unit-information and Jeffreys-BIC marginal likelihoods, complexity-penalized model priors ($r = N^{-1}$), radian-scaled physical operators, algebraic exploration without unidentifiable projection nodes ($p_{\text{proj}}=0$), a low-dimensional collinearity guard, and parallel GMJMCMC with median probability selection. Across 109,192 synthetic runs (12 systems) and 2,600 noisy-state runs (5 literature-parameterized systems) evaluated via hierarchical Linear Mixed-Effects Models (LMMs) with random system-by-method interactions, Ordered Beta GLMM sensitivity checks, and Dunnett control, tuned BGNLM achieves a synthetic false discovery rate of $0.098$ and $\text{F1}=0.852$, matching an oracle-basis Bayesian upper bound ($\text{F1}=0.859, p_{\text{adj}}=1.000$) while beating CV-BIC STLSQ, bagged STLSQ, weak-form SINDy, BAS Bayesian regression, symbolic regression, and untuned BGNLM.
\end{abstract}

% ==============================================================================
\section{Introduction}
% ==============================================================================

Inferring closed-form ordinary differential equations (ODEs) $\dot{\boldsymbol{x}}(t) = \boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x}(t))$ directly from noisy measurements is central to scientific machine learning. Sparse Identification of Nonlinear Dynamics (SINDy) \citep{brunton2016discovering} frames equation discovery as sparse linear regression $\dot{\boldsymbol{x}} \approx \boldsymbol{\Theta}(\boldsymbol{x})\boldsymbol{\Xi}$ over a fixed library $\boldsymbol{\Theta}(\boldsymbol{x})$ solved via sequentially thresholded least squares (STLSQ). Subsequent advances introduced rational libraries \citep{kaheman2020sindy}, weak-form Galerkin integration \citep{messenger2021weak}, bagging ensembles \citep{fasel2022ensemble}, and Bayesian priors \citep{hirsh2022sparsifying}. Yet dictionary-based discovery remains bound by a library dilemma: low-order polynomial bases fail when dynamics contain trigonometric, fractional, exponential, or saturating Michaelis--Menten and Hill kinetics, whereas stuffing an overcomplete dictionary creates severe multicollinearity, inflates false discovery rates (FDR), and breaks estimation when sample sizes are small.

Evolutionary Symbolic Regression (SR) frameworks such as Eureqa \citep{schmidt2009distilling}, PySR \citep{cranmer2023interpretable}, and PyOperon \citep{burlacu2020operon} avoid fixed bases by evolving expression trees over elementary operators. However, Pareto-front genetic programming yields point estimates without coherent posterior distributions over equations: it provides neither calibrated PIPs nor predictive credible intervals and suffers substantial seed-to-seed variance \citep{lacava2021contemporary}. In our earlier workshop prototype \citep{hubin2025bgnlm}, we explored Bayesian Generalized Nonlinear Models (BGNLMs) \citep{hubin2021flexible} as a basis-free alternative on three 3D systems, but that prototype used fixed dispersion ($\sigma^2=1$), degree-scaled trigonometry (\texttt{sin\_deg}/\texttt{cos\_deg}), unpruned nested projections, and an under-sized collinearity guard without comparisons against oracle Bayesian models, modern SINDy variants, SR engines, or empirical records.

In this work, we resolve these limitations via \texttt{FBMS} \citep{lachmann2025fbms} and deliver an ICML-scale methodology and empirical evaluation. We establish asymptotic model selection consistency under the GMJMCMC kernel alongside a theoretical characterization of the finite-budget large-$N$ surrogate trap. Across 109,192 synthetic runs (including held-out validation on 9 uncalibrated systems), 2,600 literature noisy-state runs, and targeted native ablations comparing Julia PySR, C++ PyOperon, weak-form SINDy, BAS Bayesian SINDy, and zero-leakage authentic field datasets, all evaluated via maximal hierarchical LMMs and Ordered Beta GLMMs, tuned BGNLM matches oracle Bayesian models while sharply cutting false discoveries.

% ==============================================================================
\section{Related Work}
% ==============================================================================

\paragraph{Sparse Dictionary Regression and UQ-SINDy.}
Since \citet{brunton2016discovering}, dictionary regression has progressed toward broader nonlinear coverage and robustness to measurement noise \citep{rudy2017data}. SINDy-PI \citep{kaheman2020sindy} accommodates rational terms via implicit left-hand-side dictionaries, at the cost of higher collinearity when cubic numerators are included. Weak SINDy (WSINDy) \citep{messenger2021weak} transfers time derivatives via integration by parts onto compactly supported $C^p$ test functions, suppressing differentiation noise on contiguous time grids. For uncertainty quantification, Ensemble-SINDy \citep{fasel2022ensemble} aggregates STLSQ supports via bootstrap bagging, while UQ-SINDy \citep{hirsh2022sparsifying} places sparsifying Bayesian priors on static library weights. Because all such methods operate inside a static matrix $\boldsymbol{\Theta}(\boldsymbol{x})$, they cannot invent absent nonlinearities or escape overcomplete collinearity.

\paragraph{Evolutionary and Neural Symbolic Regression.}
Expression-tree search optimizes structure and continuous constants concurrently \citep{schmidt2009distilling,lacava2021contemporary}. PyOperon \citep{burlacu2020operon} pairs C++ genetic programming with Levenberg--Marquardt tuning and minimum description length (MDL) selection, whereas PySR \citep{cranmer2023interpretable} evolves multi-population Julia search selected via complexity--loss Pareto knees. Physics-inspired recursive decomposition \citep{udrescu2020ai}, deep inductive-bias extraction \citep{cranmer2020discovering}, and autoencoder coordinate discovery \citep{champion2019data} further broaden scientific SR. Nevertheless, deterministic Pareto curves neither marginalize discrete structural uncertainty nor yield calibrated Bayesian error bars.

\paragraph{Bayesian Variable Selection and BGNLMs.}
Stochastic Bayesian variable selection over fixed tabular dictionaries, including Zellner $g$-priors \citep{zellner1986assessing}, mixtures of $g$-priors \citep{liang2008mixtures}, Median Probability Model (MPM) selection \citep{barbieri2004optimal}, and Bayesian Adaptive Sampling (BAS) \citep{clyde2011bayesian}, achieves principled FDR control when true terms are present in $\boldsymbol{\Theta}$. BGNLMs \citep{hubin2018mode,hubin2021flexible}, packaged in \texttt{FBMS} \citep{lachmann2025fbms}, unify symbolic feature evolution and Bayesian model averaging by embedding finite feature populations inside Genetically Modified Mode Jumping MCMC (GMJMCMC). Here we adapt and theoretically ground BGNLMs for continuous-time dynamical system identification.

% ==============================================================================
\section{BGNLM Methodology for Dynamical Systems}
% ==============================================================================

\subsection{Hierarchical State-Space and Prior Formulation}

Consider an $m$-dimensional dynamical system $\dot{\boldsymbol{x}}(t) = \boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x}(t), \boldsymbol{u}(t))$ with states $\boldsymbol{x}(t) \in \mathbb{R}^m$ and optional exogenous forcing $\boldsymbol{u}(t)$. Observing $N$ time points $\{t_i\}_{i=1}^N$ yields states $\boldsymbol{X} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times m}$ and derivative targets $Y_{ji} = \dot{x}_j(t_i) + \varepsilon_{ji}$ for equation $j \in \{1,\dots,m\}$ with $\varepsilon_{ji} \stackrel{\text{iid}}{\sim} \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma_j^2)$. Rather than fixing a static dictionary, BGNLM models each component conditionally on a dynamically evolved population $\mathcal{F}_j = \{g_1(\boldsymbol{x}), \dots, g_q(\boldsymbol{x})\} \subset \mathcal{G}_D$ of algebraic and transcendental expression trees of depth at most $D$:
\begin{equation}
  Y_{ji} \mid \boldsymbol{\gamma}_j, \boldsymbol{\beta}_j, \sigma_j^2 \sim \mathcal{N}\!\left(\beta_{j0} + \sum_{k=1}^{q} \gamma_{jk}\beta_{jk}g_k(\boldsymbol{x}_i), \; \sigma_j^2\right),
\end{equation}
where $\gamma_{jk} \in \{0,1\}$ denote binary inclusion indicators and $\boldsymbol{\beta}_{j,\boldsymbol{\gamma}_j}$ denotes active weights. Given $\boldsymbol{X}$, equations factorize conditionally and can be estimated in parallel.

Following \citet{hubin2021flexible,lachmann2025fbms}, each nonlinear feature $g_k \in \mathcal{G}_D$ carries structural complexity $c(g_k) \ge 1$. At GMJMCMC generation $p$, we assign a hierarchical Bernoulli prior,
\begin{equation}
  p(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_j \mid \mathcal{F}_j^{(p)}) \propto \prod_{k=1}^{q} r^{c(g_k)\,\gamma_{jk}}, \qquad r = N^{-1},
\end{equation}
so adding an atom of complexity $c(g_k)$ incurs an explicit log-prior penalty $-c(g_k)\log N$. Within each generation, model weights are normalized over $\mathcal{F}_j^{(p)}$ and aggregated across populations proportionally to marginal likelihood mass. Our primary unknown-variance Unit-Information / Jeffreys-BIC formulation pairs a proper unit-information parameter prior \citep{kass1995reference} with $p(\sigma_j^2) \propto \sigma_j^{-2}$, yielding $\log p(\boldsymbol{y}_j \mid \boldsymbol{\gamma}_j) = -\frac{N}{2}\log(\text{RSS}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}_j}/N) - \frac{p_{\gamma}+1}{2}\log N + O(1)$. Estimating $\sigma_j^2$ adapts automatically across $\sigma \in [10^{-3}, 1.0]$, overcoming the severe calibration error of fixed $\sigma_j^2=1$ defaults \citep{hubin2025bgnlm}. We also compare Zellner's $g$-prior ($g=\max(N,p^2)$) \citep{zellner1986assessing} and mixtures of $g$-priors (\texttt{robust} tCCH and \texttt{hyper-g/n}) \citep{liang2008mixtures}.

\subsection{Identifiability, Collinearity Fix, and Theory}

Reliable ODE discovery with \texttt{FBMS} requires three structural safeguards. First, we disable linear-combination projection mutations ($p_{\text{proj}} = 0$, retaining algebraic interactions $p_{\text{inter}} = 0.55$, unary mutations $p_{\text{mod}} = 0.35$, and re-seeding $p_{\text{new}} = 0.10$ at depth $D \le 2$), preventing unidentifiable nested compositions like $\sin(1+\sum \alpha_l g_l)$. Second, we replace degree-scaled trigonometry (\texttt{sin\_deg}, which evaluates $\sin(\pi x/180)$) with exact radian and rational operators (\texttt{sin\_rad}, \texttt{cos\_rad}, \texttt{p2}, \texttt{p3}, \texttt{p05}, \texttt{pm1}, \texttt{exp\_neg}, \texttt{inv\_pos}, \texttt{inv\_sq}, \texttt{hill\_2}, \texttt{sigmoid}). Third, we patch \texttt{FBMS:::check.collinearity}: because its default subsample $n_{\text{col}} = 2p_0$ ($p_0 = m$) triggers rank-deficiency stalls as soon as $|\mathcal{F}_j| \ge 4$ on 2D/3D ODEs, expanding $n_{\text{col}} = \min(N, \max(2p_0, 3|\mathcal{F}_j|+15))$ and removing zero-variance inputs restores full search exploration.

\begin{proposition}[Asymptotic Model Selection Consistency]
Suppose the true active set $\mathcal{S}_j^\star \subset \mathcal{G}_D$ of equation $j$ is reachable at depth $D$ with strictly positive proposal probability under the collinearity-guarded GMJMCMC kernel for $\text{pop.max} \ge |\mathcal{S}_j^\star|$, with $\varepsilon_{ji} \stackrel{\text{iid}}{\sim} \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma_j^2)$ and full-rank $\boldsymbol{G}_{\mathcal{S}_j^\star}$. Under unit-information / Jeffreys-BIC or Zellner $g$-prior ($g=\max(N,p^2)$) marginal likelihoods paired with $r = N^{-\kappa}$ ($\kappa > 0$), as $N \to \infty$, every misspecified or overfitted model $\boldsymbol{\gamma}_j \neq \boldsymbol{\gamma}_j^\star$ satisfies $\log \mathrm{BF}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}_j^\star : \boldsymbol{\gamma}_j) \to +\infty$ in probability, and $\lim_{N\to\infty} P(\hat{\mathcal{S}}_j^{\text{MPM}} = \mathcal{S}_j^\star \mid \boldsymbol{D}) = 1$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof Sketch]
Models omitting a true atom suffer residual floor inflation $\Delta_{\text{bias}} > 0$, yielding an $\Omega(N)$ Bayes factor deficit against $\boldsymbol{\gamma}_j^\star$. Conversely, adding $k$ spurious atoms of complexities $\{c_l\}$ yields $O_p(1)$ log-likelihood gain by Wilks' theorem against a deterministic prior penalty $\sum_{l=1}^k (c_l + \tfrac{1}{2})\log N \to +\infty$. Irreducibility of the collinearity-guarded GMJMCMC kernel over $\mathcal{G}_D$ concentrates marginal mass onto $\boldsymbol{\gamma}_j^\star$, driving $\widehat{\text{PIP}}_{jk} \to \mathbb{I}(g_k \in \mathcal{S}_j^\star)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Fixed-Budget MCMC Surrogate Trap at Large $N$]
Under a fixed finite chain budget $(P, N_{\text{MCMC}})$, a multi-term Taylor or polynomial surrogate reducing residual MSE over sub-models accrues $O(N)$ log-likelihood advantage versus $O(\log N)$ complexity cost. When $N \gg N_{\text{MCMC}}$, this sharpening traps finite chains in bloated surrogate modes before exact rational or transcendental atoms are visited, inflating FDR at $N=2500$ unless MCMC search budgets scale alongside adapted complexity decay $r = N^{-1/2}$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Posterior Selection, Calibration, and Algorithm}

\begin{algorithm}[t]
\caption{Tuned BGNLM-SINDy via Parallel GMJMCMC}
\label{alg:bgnlm_sindy}
\begin{small}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require States $\boldsymbol{X}$, derivatives $\boldsymbol{Y}$, transforms $\mathcal{T}_{\text{ext}}$, $r=N^{-1}$.
\State Filter zero-variance columns ($s_l \le 10^{-12}$) from $\boldsymbol{X}$.
\For{each equation $j = 1, \dots, m$}
  \State Run $R=6$ parallel GMJMCMC chains ($P=12, N_{\text{MCMC}}=500$, $p_{\text{gen}}=(0.55, 0.35, 0.00, 0.10)$, $D \le 2$, $\text{pop.max}=20$, unknown-$\sigma_j^2$ Jeffreys-BIC).
  \State Weight populations by marginal likelihood to obtain $\widehat{\text{PIP}}_{jk}$ and BMA predictive quantiles.
  \State Select the Median Probability Model $\hat{\mathcal{S}}_j^{\text{MPM}} = \{g_k : \widehat{\text{PIP}}_{jk} > 0.5\}$ and refit final OLS mean.
\EndFor
\State \Return Discovered sets $\{\hat{\mathcal{S}}_j^{\text{MPM}}\}$, $\widehat{\text{PIP}}_{jk}$, and Student-$t$ hybrid intervals.
\end{algorithmic}
\end{small}
\end{algorithm}

Following Median Probability Model (MPM) selection ($\widehat{\text{PIP}}_{jk} > 0.5$) \citep{barbieri2004optimal}, we refit OLS on the selected active features $\hat{\mathcal{S}}_j^{\text{MPM}}$ without any post-hoc coefficient thresholding, preserving direct equivalence with Proposition~1. Hybrid 95\% predictive intervals combine BMA quantile half-widths $h_{\text{BMA}}(\boldsymbol{x}_*)$ and post-selection Student-$t$ dispersion $t_{0.975, df}\hat{s}_*$ in quadrature (Algorithm~\ref{alg:bgnlm_sindy}). Calibrating 8 configurations on a 6-task slice (\texttt{sys02}, \texttt{sys04}, \texttt{sys08} at $N=250$, $\sigma \in \{0.01,0.25\}$, seed $101$; Table~\ref{tab:bgnlm_tuning} in Appendix~\ref{app:systems}) locked configuration \texttt{C04} (Jeffreys-BIC, unknown $\sigma_j^2$, $r=N^{-1}$, $p_{\text{proj}}=0$), lifting calibration F1 from $0.684$ to $0.976$ and cutting Brier error 20-fold ($0.227 \to 0.011$); Section~\ref{sec:exp1_results} verifies zero tuning leakage on all 9 unseen systems.

% ==============================================================================
\section{Experimental Protocol \& Statistical Evaluation}
% ==============================================================================

\subsection{Baselines, AST Canonicalization, and Metrics}

We benchmark 13 core configurations across four families (raw RDS/CSV mappings in Appendix~\ref{app:systems}): basis-free BGNLM (\texttt{BGNLM\_Full\_Tuned}, $\mathcal{P}_2$-seeded \texttt{BGNLM\_Hybrid\_Poly}, and untuned fixed-variance \texttt{BGNLM\_IWSM\_Untuned}); predefined-basis BGLMs running identical \texttt{FBMS} MJMCMC inference (\texttt{BGLM\_Oracle\_Basis} with $\mathcal{P}_3(\boldsymbol{X})$ plus exact non-polynomial ground-truth atoms, misspecified \texttt{BGLM\_Poly\_Basis} over $\mathcal{P}_3(\boldsymbol{X})$, and collinear 60--275-column \texttt{BGLM\_Overcomplete}); SINDy baselines (CV-BIC STLSQ \texttt{SINDy\_STLSQ\_Oracle}/\texttt{\_Poly}, 80-replicate bagged \texttt{Bag\_STLSQ\_Oracle}/\texttt{\_Poly}, and explicit rational \texttt{SINDy\_RatPoly2}); and static-pool SR proxies (\texttt{SR\_Pool\_Pareto}, \texttt{SR\_Pool\_MDL}), supplemented in targeted native follow-ups by native Julia PySR, native C++ PyOperon, corrected standardized-$\hat{\lambda}^*$ \texttt{Bag\_STLSQ\_Poly}, BAS Bayesian SINDy (\texttt{UQ\_SINDy\_Bayes}), and Galerkin Weak-SINDy (\texttt{WSINDy}).

An AST canonicalizer expands algebraic products and powers up to degree 6 to extract canonical non-constant atoms $\hat{\mathcal{S}}_j$ (unpenalized intercepts capture constant forcings). Under our audited scoring rules, degree-scaled \texttt{sin\_deg}/\texttt{cos\_deg} are never aliased to radian \texttt{sin}/\texttt{cos}, rational complements modulo intercept ($x^2/(1+x^2) \equiv 1 - 1/(1+x^2)$ and $x/(1+x) \equiv 1 - 1/(1+x)$) apply symmetrically across all methods, and crashed runs (\texttt{Status != "OK"}) receive $\text{F1}=\text{TPR}=R^2=0$ with \texttt{FDR}, \texttt{OC}, and \texttt{SHD} marked \texttt{NA}. We report structural F1, TPR, FDR, Exact Recovery, SHD, Operation Count (OC), shared-dimension ($d=100$) Nogueira stability $\hat{\Phi}$ \citep{nogueira2018stability}, PIP calibration (Brier, denominator-invariant SSPE, AUROC, and reliability curves), clipped $R^2_{\text{ood}}$, continuous-input RK4 trajectory rollout $R^2_{\text{traj}}$, and 95\% predictive interval coverage.

\subsection{Benchmark Suites and Mixed-Effects Models}

Experiment~1 spans 12 ODEs integrated via \texttt{lsoda} ($\Delta t = 10^{-4}$) across 7 polynomial systems (Group~A: Linear-Quadratic 3D, Lorenz-63, R\"ossler, Lotka--Volterra, Van der Pol, Duffing, Brusselator), 4 non-polynomial systems (Group~B: R\"ossler--Lorenz Hybrid, Pendulum, Thomas Attractor, Michaelis--Menten Goodwin), and 5D Lorenz-96 (Group~C), crossed with $N \in \{100, 250, 500, 1000, 2500\}$, 7 derivative SNR noise levels $\sigma \in \{0.001, \dots, 1.0\}$, 20 seeds, and 13 methods (109,192 completed runs; 100\% completion for 12/13 methods and 656 crashes exclusively in \texttt{BGLM\_Overcomplete}). Experiment~2 tests noisy-state differentiation on 5 literature-parameterized benchmarks (Lynx--Hare, \emph{Paramecium--Didinium}, Pendulum, Cascaded Tanks, Yeast Glycolysis) across 20 rolling 70\%/30\% folds and 2 smoothers (Savitzky--Golay spline vs.\ Chartrand TVRegDiff, 2,600 runs). Targeted Jobs 1--4 examine pure MPM vs.\ pruned MPM, $N=2500$ budget scaling, state noise, native PySR/PyOperon/BAS/WSINDy, non-unit scales, and strictly post-split zero-leakage empirical datasets.

To respect hierarchical clustering and heterogeneous cross-system performance, we fit maximal LMMs via \texttt{lme4}/\texttt{lmerTest} \citep{bates2015fitting,kuznetsova2017lmertest} with \texttt{BGNLM\_Full\_Tuned} as reference:
\begin{multline}
  Y_{mjs} = \beta_0 + \sum_{k \neq \text{ref}} \delta_k \mathbb{I}(m=k) + \boldsymbol{\eta}^\top \boldsymbol{z}_s \\
  + u_{\text{Sys}[j]} + w_{\text{Sys:M}[j,m]} + v_{\text{Seed}[j,s]} + \varepsilon_{mjs},
\end{multline}
where $\boldsymbol{z}_s = (\log_{10}\sigma_s, \log_{10}N_s)^\top$ (or smoother indicator in Experiment~2), $u_{\text{Sys}[j]}$ models baseline system difficulty, $w_{\text{Sys:M}[j,m]}$ captures cross-system method heterogeneity (yielding conservative Satterthwaite $df \approx 132.0$ and $48.0$), and $v_{\text{Seed}[j,s]}$ pairs replicates. We report Dunnett multiplicity-adjusted contrasts $p_{\text{adj}}$ \citep{dunnett1955multiple,lenth2024emmeans}, within-regime Dunnett contrasts, a binomial exact-recovery GLMM with $(1\mid\text{System:Seed\_ID})$, and Ordered Beta GLMM sensitivity models (\texttt{glmmTMB::ordbeta}) \citep{brooks2017glmmtmb,kubinec2023ordered}.

% ==============================================================================
\section{Empirical Results}
% ==============================================================================

\subsection{Experiment 1: Synthetic Benchmark (109,192 Runs)}
\label{sec:exp1_results}

Figure~\ref{fig:synth_overview}, Table~\ref{tab:synth_overall}, and Table~\ref{tab:synth_regimes} summarize overall and regime-level results (mixed-effects forest plots and full contrast tables appear in Appendix~\ref{app:lmm_tables}). Under cross-system LMM inference ($df = 132.2, \text{SE}=0.067$), \texttt{BGNLM\_Full\_Tuned} achieves $\text{F1}=0.852, \text{TPR}=0.860, \text{Exact}=64.5\%, R^2_{\text{OOD}}=0.888$, statistically tied with the oracle upper bound \texttt{BGLM\_Oracle\_Basis} across F1 ($\Delta=0.007, p=0.921, p_{\text{adj}}=1.000$), FDR ($p_{\text{adj}}=1.000$), $R^2_{\text{OOD}}$ ($p_{\text{adj}}=1.000$), and $R^2_{\text{traj}}$ ($p_{\text{adj}}=1.000$). Tuned BGNLM yields the lowest FDR across all 13 methods ($\text{FDR}=0.098$), significantly outperforming \texttt{SINDy\_RatPoly2} ($+0.509, p_{\text{adj}}<10^{-4}$), \texttt{Bag\_STLSQ\_Poly} ($+0.392, p_{\text{adj}}=0.0002$), \texttt{SINDy\_STLSQ\_Poly} ($+0.335, p_{\text{adj}}=0.0024$), and \texttt{SR\_Pool\_MDL} ($+0.273, p_{\text{adj}}=0.0235$) under Dunnett multiplicity adjustment, and delivers large Dunnett F1 gains over \texttt{SINDy\_RatPoly2} ($+0.409, p_{\text{adj}}<10^{-4}$), \texttt{Bag\_STLSQ\_Poly} ($+0.357, p_{\text{adj}}<10^{-4}$), \texttt{SR\_Pool\_Pareto}/\texttt{\_MDL} ($+0.296$ to $+0.313, p_{\text{adj}} \le 0.0002$), \texttt{BGNLM\_IWSM\_Untuned} ($+0.290, p_{\text{adj}}=0.0003$), \texttt{SINDy\_STLSQ\_Poly} ($+0.252, p_{\text{adj}}=0.0025$), and \texttt{BGLM\_Overcomplete} ($+0.235, p_{\text{adj}}=0.0062$). Ordered Beta GLMM sensitivity models (Table~\ref{tab:ordbeta_sensitivity}) confirm 100\% concordance, exact-recovery binomial GLMM log-odds rise by $+7.50$ to $+12.40$ ($p_{\text{adj}} \le 0.0023$), and strict evaluation on the 9 held-out uncalibrated systems preserves $\text{F1}=0.814, \text{FDR}=0.126, R^2_{\text{OOD}}=0.855$ (tied with oracle $0.832, df=96.0, p_{\text{adj}}=1.000$).

Across dynamical regimes (Table~\ref{tab:synth_regimes}), \texttt{BGNLM\_Full\_Tuned} ranks first on Group~A polynomials ($\text{F1}=0.863, \text{Exact}=71.3\%, \text{FDR}=0.044$) without paying the cubic library overhead of $\mathcal{P}_3$. On Group~B non-polynomials, misspecified polynomial methods fail completely ($0.0\%$ exact recovery, $\text{F1}=0.291$--$0.436$), whereas tuned BGNLM recovers trigonometric and Hill forms ($\text{F1}=0.800$, $48.7\%$ exact recovery, doubling \texttt{SINDy\_RatPoly2} on Goodwin MM). On 5D Lorenz-96 (Group~C), tuned BGNLM ranks first among all 13 methods ($\text{F1}=0.983, \text{Exact}=80.6\%, R^2_{\text{traj}}=0.958$), while \texttt{BGLM\_Overcomplete} crashes on 656 Group~B/C tasks due to static collinearity.

\end{table*}

Across Experiment~1, F1 rises with $\log_{10} N$ ($\hat{\beta}=+0.054, t=28.54, p<10^{-4}$) while FDR falls ($\hat{\beta}=-0.031, t=-14.12, p<10^{-4}$). Consistent with Remark~1, fixed-budget \texttt{BGNLM\_Full\_Tuned} peaks at $N=500$ ($\text{F1}=0.889, \text{FDR}=0.057$) before declining to $0.762$ at $N=2500$ ($\text{FDR}=0.212$), while remaining resilient up to $\sigma=0.5$ ($\text{F1}=0.838$) and $\sigma=1.0$ ($0.778$, surpassing oracle \texttt{BGLM\_Oracle\_Basis} at $0.725$). Figure~\ref{fig:uq_calibration} highlights top Nogueira stability ($\hat{\Phi}=0.884$) and well-calibrated PIPs (Brier $0.0898$, $92.6\%$ empirical 95\% coverage), while Table~\ref{tab:qual_equations} and Figure~\ref{fig:qualitative_rollouts} confirm clean symbolic structure recovery and coherent autonomous rollouts.

\subsection{Targeted Native Baselines and Expressivity Ablations}
\label{sec:targeted_ablation}

To evaluate large-$N$ budget scaling (Remark~1), native Julia PySR and C++ PyOperon backends, corrected CV-$\hat{\lambda}^*$ \texttt{Bag\_STLSQ\_Poly}, BAS Bayesian dictionary averaging, Galerkin WSINDy, and non-unit internal scale expressivity under pure Bayesian MPM ($\widehat{\text{PIP}}_{jk} > 0.5$), we executed targeted ablations in \texttt{scripts/run\_targeted\_ablation.R} (Table~\ref{tab:ablation_native}). Across 9 BMA vs.\ MPM-OLS checks, posterior predictive accuracy matches tightly ($R^2_{\text{OOD}}=0.996$ vs.\ $0.995$). Scaling search budgets ($P=16, N_{\text{MCMC}}=800$) with $r=N^{-1/2}$ on Goodwin MM lifts F1 from $0.742 \to 0.871$ at $N=500$, from $0.635 \to 0.767$ ($\text{Exact}: 0.0 \to 0.500$) at $N=1000$, and from $0.458 \to 0.485$ ($R^2_{\text{traj}}: 0.465 \to 0.628$) at $N=2500$, while holding $\text{F1}=1.000$ on Lorenz-63.

In fair head-to-head runs ($N=300, \eta=0.05$, Table~\ref{tab:ablation_native}), pure-MPM \texttt{BGNLM\_Full\_Tuned} ranks first under both \texttt{derivative\_snr} ($\text{F1}=0.962, \text{SSPE}=0.461, \text{AUROC}=0.979$) and \texttt{state\_noise} ($\text{F1}=0.696, \text{FDR}=0.359$), ahead of oracle BAS (\texttt{UQ\_SINDy\_Bayes\_Oracle}, $\text{F1}=0.919$ / $0.502$), native Julia \texttt{SR\_PySR} ($0.784$ / $0.452$), fixed-$\hat{\lambda}^*$ \texttt{Bag\_STLSQ\_Poly} ($0.616$ / $0.324$), native \texttt{SR\_Operon} ($0.527$ / $0.439$), and degree-3 \texttt{SINDy\_PI\_Rat} ($0.445$ / $0.245$), while \texttt{WSINDy\_Oracle} achieves top noisy-state rollout ($R^2_{\text{traj}}=0.600$ vs.\ $0.341$) by integrating by parts. Removing invalid \texttt{sin\_deg} aliasing drops \texttt{BGNLM\_IWSM\_Untuned} to $0.0\%$ exact recovery on trigonometric systems ($R^2_{\text{traj}}=0.316$ vs.\ $0.832$ on Pendulum). Finally, on non-unit internal scale stress tests, tuned BGNLM ($p_{\text{proj}}=0$) discovers the double-angle factorization $2\sin(x_1)\cos(x_1)$ on $\ddot{\theta} = -0.25\dot{\theta}-5\sin(2\theta)$ ($R^2_{\text{traj}}=1.000$, surpassing native PySR at $0.964$ and PyOperon at $0.975$) and achieves $\text{F1}=0.846, R^2_{\text{traj}}=0.999$ on Michaelis--Menten $K_m=0.3$ kinetics, whereas enabling $p_{\text{proj}}=0.05$ lowers F1 to $0.600$.

& \multicolumn{5}{c}{\textbf{Overall Zero-Leakage Summary}} & \multicolumn{5}{c}{\textbf{Per-Dataset Structural F1 ($R^2_{\text{traj}}$)}} \\
\cmidrule(lr){2-6}\cmidrule(lr){7-11}
Method & F1 $\uparrow$ & FDR $\downarrow$ & OC $\downarrow$ & $R^2_{\text{OOD}}$ & $R^2_{\text{traj}}$ & Lynx-Hare & Pendulum & Yeast (Osc.) & Huffaker & Gause \\
\midrule
\textbf{\texttt{BGNLM\_Full\_Tuned}} & \textbf{0.499} & \textbf{0.255} & 10.4 & 0.460 & \textbf{0.454} & 0.551 (\textbf{0.61}) & \textbf{1.000} (\textbf{1.00}) & \textbf{0.850} (\textbf{0.66}) & 0.095 (0.00) & 0.000 (0.00) \\
\texttt{SR\_PySR} (Native) & 0.302 & 0.544 & \textbf{9.8} & 0.488 & 0.384 & \textbf{0.617} (0.58) & 0.821 (0.99) & 0.074 (0.35) & 0.000 (0.00) & 0.000 (0.00) \\
\texttt{Bag\_STLSQ\_Poly} (Fixed) & 0.271 & 0.603 & 20.8 & 0.471 & 0.399 & 0.401 (\textbf{0.74}) & 0.487 (0.99) & 0.195 (0.40) & \textbf{0.312} (0.00) & 0.000 (0.00) \\
\texttt{UQ\_SINDy\_Bayes\_Poly} & 0.237 & 0.739 & 20.2 & \textbf{0.516} & 0.369 & 0.245 (0.33) & 0.347 (0.99) & 0.369 (0.52) & 0.222 (0.00) & 0.000 (0.00) \\
\texttt{WSINDy\_Poly} & 0.232 & 0.432 & 14.7 & 0.457 & 0.422 & 0.369 (0.48) & 0.372 (0.99) & 0.301 (0.52) & 0.000 (0.00) & 0.000 (0.00) \\
\texttt{SINDy\_STLSQ\_Poly} & 0.227 & 0.512 & 16.4 & 0.472 & 0.389 & 0.308 (0.43) & 0.390 (0.99) & 0.306 (0.52) & 0.133 (0.00) & 0.000 (0.00) \\
\texttt{SR\_Operon} (Native) & 0.217 & 0.711 & 24.3 & 0.488 & 0.425 & 0.397 (0.47) & 0.500 (1.00) & 0.261 (0.64) & 0.000 (0.00) & 0.000 (0.00) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}%
}
\end{table*}

On the 5 literature-parameterized benchmarks (Table~\ref{tab:real_overall}), \texttt{BGNLM\_Full\_Tuned} ranks first among all basis-free methods ($\text{F1}=0.624$, lowest valid $\text{FDR}=0.416$, lowest valid $\text{OC}=10.6$), matching \texttt{BGLM\_Oracle\_Basis} ($p=0.595, p_{\text{adj}}=0.990$) and surpassing \texttt{Bag\_STLSQ\_Poly} ($+0.344, p_{\text{adj}}=0.044$) and polynomial SINDy/BGLM ($p \le 0.014$) with $92.5\%$ coverage. Under strictly post-split zero-leakage differentiation on authentic empirical series and sustained-oscillation Yeast Glycolysis (Table~\ref{tab:authentic_real}), tuned BGNLM leads overall ($\text{F1}=0.499, \text{FDR}=0.255, R^2_{\text{traj}}=0.454$), outperforming native PySR ($0.302$), fixed bagged STLSQ ($0.271$), BAS ($0.237$), WSINDy ($0.232$), and PyOperon ($0.217$), achieving $1.000$ F1 on Pendulum and $0.850$ F1 ($R^2_{\text{traj}}=0.660$) on sustained Yeast Glycolysis while highlighting open challenges on ultra-sparse 24-point Gause and noisy 60-week Huffaker data.

% ==============================================================================
\section{Discussion, Limitations, and Conclusion}
% ==============================================================================

Deterministic SINDy runs in $1.9$--$2.8\,\text{s}$ per system whereas 6-chain GMJMCMC averages $235.4\,\text{s}$ (synthetic) and $137.3\,\text{s}$ (literature) per multi-equation ODE, trading compute for basis-free recovery, minimal FDR, and calibrated uncertainty. Across all fitted LMMs (Table~\ref{tab:mixed_effects_icc}, all converging with \texttt{Singular = No}), \texttt{System:Method} and \texttt{Dataset:Method} interactions account for $25.1\%$--$39.5\%$ of synthetic F1/FDR variance and $33.7\%$ of empirical F1 variance, underscoring why cross-system random interactions are essential. Three limitations remain: derivative regression weakens near rapid equilibrium decay (\texttt{sys01}) or high state noise where integral WSINDy excels; fixed MCMC budgets require co-scaling at large $N=2500$ (Remark~1); and $p_{\text{proj}}=0$ prioritizes identifiability over arbitrary internal scalar frequencies. Overall, by uniting collinearity-guarded GMJMCMC with unknown-variance Jeffreys-BIC priors and radian/rational expressivity, tuned BGNLMs match oracle Bayesian inference across 12 synthetic ODEs and noisy empirical records while eliminating the dictionary dilemma.

\section{Dynamical Systems, Calibration Sweep, and Literature Benchmarks}
\label{app:systems}

For exact checkpoint reproducibility, archived RDS/CSV labels map directly to paper names: \texttt{SR\_PySR} = \texttt{SR\_Pool\_Pareto}, \texttt{SR\_Operon} = \texttt{SR\_Pool\_MDL}, \texttt{UQ\_SINDy\_Oracle/Poly} = \texttt{Bag\_STLSQ\_Oracle/Poly}, and \texttt{SINDy\_PI\_Rational} = \texttt{SINDy\_RatPoly2}. Table~\ref{tab:bgnlm_tuning} reports the 8-configuration calibration sweep, and Tables~\ref{tab:systems_spec} and \ref{tab:real_spec} specify all 12 synthetic systems and 5 literature benchmarks.

\begin{table*}[t]
\centering
\caption{Canonical Synthetic Benchmark Dynamical Systems (Groups A, B, and C). Non-constant atoms define structural active sets; intercepts ($b,a,c,F$) are unpenalized.}
\label{tab:systems_spec}
\scriptsize
\resizebox{\textwidth}{!}{%
\begin{tabular}{cllll}
\toprule
ID & System (Group) & Governing Equations & Parameters & Initial Cond. $\boldsymbol{x}(0)$ \\
\midrule
1 & Linear-Quad 3D (A) & $\dot{x}_1 = ax_1 + bx_1x_2$, $\dot{x}_2 = cx_1 + dx_1x_2$, $\dot{x}_3 = ex_3$ & $a=-1,b=20,c=-20,d=-1,e=-3$ & $(2,0,1)$ \\
2 & Lorenz-63 (A) & $\dot{x}_1 = \sigma(x_2-x_1)$, $\dot{x}_2 = x_1(\rho-x_3)-x_2$, $\dot{x}_3 = x_1x_2-\beta x_3$ & $\sigma=10,\rho=28,\beta=8/3$ & $(-0.5,-2,3)$ \\
3 & R\"ossler (A) & $\dot{x}_1 = -x_2-x_3$, $\dot{x}_2 = x_1+ax_2$, $\dot{x}_3 = b+x_3(x_1-c)$ & $a=0.2,b=0.2,c=5.7$ & $(0.5,-1,2)$ \\
4 & Lotka--Volterra (A) & $\dot{x}_1 = \alpha x_1 - \beta x_1x_2$, $\dot{x}_2 = \delta x_1x_2 - \gamma x_2$ & $\alpha=1.5,\beta=1,\delta=1,\gamma=3$ & $(1,1)$ \\
5 & Van der Pol (A) & $\dot{x}_1 = x_2$, $\dot{x}_2 = \mu(1-x_1^2)x_2 - x_1$ & $\mu=2.0$ & $(2,0)$ \\
6 & Duffing (A) & $\dot{x}_1 = x_2$, $\dot{x}_2 = -\delta x_2 - \alpha x_1 - \beta x_1^3$ & $\delta=0.1,\alpha=-1,\beta=1$ & $(0.5,1)$ \\
7 & Brusselator (A) & $\dot{x}_1 = a - (b+1)x_1 + x_1^2x_2$, $\dot{x}_2 = bx_1 - x_1^2x_2$ & $a=1.0,b=3.0$ & $(1.5,3)$ \\
\midrule
8 & R\"ossler--Lorenz Hyb. (B) & $\dot{x}_1 = -x_2-x_3+a\sin(x_1)$, $\dot{x}_2 = x_1+bx_2$, $\dot{x}_3 = c+x_3(x_1-d)$ & $a=0.2,b=0.1,c=0.4,d=5.7$ & $(0.5,-1,2)$ \\
9 & Damped Pendulum (B) & $\dot{x}_1 = x_2$, $\dot{x}_2 = -\gamma x_2 - (g/L)\sin(x_1)$ & $\gamma=0.25, g/L=5.0$ & $(3.0416,0)$ \\
10 & Thomas Attractor (B) & $\dot{x}_i = \sin(x_{(i \bmod 3)+1}) - b x_i$, $i=1,2,3$ & $b=0.18$ & $(0.1,0,0)$ \\
11 & Goodwin MM (B) & $\dot{x}_1 = \frac{v_0}{1+x_3^p}-k_1x_1$, $\dot{x}_2 = k_1x_1-k_2x_2$, $\dot{x}_3 = k_2x_2-\frac{v_m x_3}{K_m+|x_3|}$ & $v_0=v_m=K_m=1, p=2, k_1=k_2=0.5$ & $(0.5,0.5,0.5)$ \\
\midrule
12 & Lorenz-96 5D (C) & $\dot{x}_i = (x_{i+1}-x_{i-2})x_{i-1} - x_i + F$, $i=1,\dots,5$ & $F=8.0$ & $(8,8,8,8,8.01)$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}%
}
\end{table*}
